Part 4 — Licence Types

PART 4 — LICENCE TYPES

4.1 Regulator and legal basis

  • Regulator: the Alcohol and Marijuana Control Office (AMCO), Department of Commerce, Community, and Economic Development (DCCED); licensing decisions are made by the five-member Marijuana Control Board (MCB), described on AMCO’s home page as “a regulatory and quasi-judicial agency for the control of the cultivation, manufacture, and sale of marijuana in the state.” Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/home.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01.
  • Governing law/regulation cited throughout: AS 17.38 and 3 AAC 306. AMCO’s licence-application page directs applicants to “Review Title 17.38 of Alaska Statutes and Chapter 306 of the Alaska Administrative Code.” Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MarijuanaLicenseApplication.aspx — via search result, accessed 2026-11-01 (see §5.1 for the database-transition caveat on this page family).
  • Licence term: one year. The 2026–27 renewal deadline was 30 June 2026, with a final cut-off of 31 August 2026. Source: CannBus, Alaska Cannabis Market Intelligence Report — Q3 2026 (data as of 30 September 2026, secondary synthesis of AMCO documents): https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01.
  • No statewide cap, lottery or application window: secondary summaries of AMCO guidance consistently state there is no statewide cap; local governments may ban, cap or protest licences. This is consistent with the local-option structure, but the absence of a cap was NOT confirmed by reading a current 3 AAC 306 cap provision in this run — mark the “no cap” proposition Verify against the current 3 AAC 306 text before relying on it for an application. Sources: https://www.cannbus.org/lsum/ak_lsum.html and https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01.
  • No separate licence classes exist for: wholesale/distribution/transport, delivery to homes, microbusiness, or medical dispensary. Cultivators sell/transfer directly to retail stores and manufacturers; packages are tracked in Metrc; curbside pickup is noted in secondary sources but no delivery licence or endorsement appears in AMCO’s licence types or fee schedule. Source: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01. Verify the current delivery/curbside position in 3 AAC 306 before advising a client, as a September 2026 MCB rule package (see §4.8) was still in Department of Law review / public comment and was not final.

4.2 Licence types at a glance

Licence / permit What it allows (plain English) Annual licence fee — NEW Annual licence fee — RENEWAL (see fee conflict note) Application fee Canopy / scope limit
Standard marijuana cultivation facility Cultivate, harvest, package, store, label marijuana; sell only to licensed retail stores, manufacturers or other cultivators $5,000 $7,000 under pre-2025 3 AAC 306.100(d) — see note $1,000 non-refundable More than 500 sq ft of cultivation area
Limited marijuana cultivation facility Same as standard, at small scale $1,000 $1,400 under pre-2025 3 AAC 306.100(d) — see note $1,000 non-refundable No more than 500 sq ft
Standard marijuana product manufacturing facility Manufacture marijuana products including concentrates, edibles and topicals (food-safety permit required in secondary summaries) $5,000 $7,000 under pre-2025 3 AAC 306.100(d) — see note $1,000 non-refundable No canopy limit; product scope is the limit
Marijuana concentrate manufacturing facility (“limited” manufacturing) Manufacture concentrates only $1,000 $2,000 under pre-2025 3 AAC 306.100(d) — see note $1,000 non-refundable Concentrates only
Marijuana testing facility Independently test marijuana/marijuana products; may NOT hold an interest in any other marijuana licence $1,000 $5,000 under pre-2025 3 AAC 306.100(d) — see note $1,000 non-refundable No canopy; independence is the limit
Retail marijuana store Sell flower, concentrates, edibles and infused products to adults 21+ and registered medical patients (patients buy through the same stores; there is no separate medical store licence) $5,000 $7,000 under pre-2025 3 AAC 306.100(d) — see note $1,000 non-refundable Purchase limits per person per day apply at the store (see Part 1 of the full dossier, not this report)
Onsite consumption endorsement (add-on to a retail store licence only, NOT a standalone licence) Operate a designated consumption area attached to the retail store $2,000 endorsement fee $2,000 (endorsement renewal stated in secondary fee tables) $1,000 Designated area only; see §4.7
Marijuana handler permit (individual worker permit, NOT an establishment licence) Required for each licensee, employee or agent who sells, cultivates, manufactures, tests or transports marijuana/marijuana product, or checks ID of a consumer/visitor (3 AAC 306.700) $50 (3-year permit) Renewal: retake approved course + $50 (secondary) Included in the $50 Person-level, not premises-level

Sources for the fee table: AMCO Marijuana License Application fee schedule (new fees $5,000/$1,000 as listed; renewal column as crawled shows $5,000/$1,000 — see conflict note below): https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MarijuanaLicenseApplication.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01; 3 AAC 306.100(d) renewal fees as reproduced in secondary summaries and in the 2025 board proposal document: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/Portals/9/pub/Archive26/MCB/Minutes/2025/3.14.25/TAB%203-%20Reduced%20License%20Renewal%20Fees.pdf — accessed 2026-11-01; handler permit page: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MarijuanaHandlerPermit.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01.

FEE CONFLICT — READ BEFORE USING THE TABLE (important). Two different renewal-fee pictures are in circulation and this report does NOT resolve which one was in force on 1 November 2026: 1. The long-standing 3 AAC 306.100(d) text reproduced in multiple secondary sources and in the March 2025 board packet pre-2025 schedule (superseded) set renewal fees HIGHER than new fees: retail/standard cultivation/standard product manufacturing $7,000; limited cultivation $1,400; concentrate manufacturing $2,000; testing $5,000. 2. A board proposal considered in 2024–2025 would cut every renewal fee to the NEW-licence level ($5,000/$1,000 across the board). The proposal document shows the amendment in strikethrough form, e.g. “for a renewed retail marijuana store license, $5,000 [$7,000]”. The AMCO application page as crawled for this report lists renewal fees at the $5,000/$1,000 level, which is consistent with the proposal having been adopted — but this report did NOT open a current, in-force 3 AAC 306.100(d) text or an adoption notice confirming it. Treatment adopted here: new-licence fees are stated as confirmed; renewal fees are stated as the pre-2025 text with this conflict flagged. Verify the current 3 AAC 306.100(d) (Register citation and effective date) with AMCO before budgeting a renewal. The same caution applies to the $600 renewal application fee and the $1,000 late-renewal fee after 30 June 2026 reported in secondary sources — both are consistent with board documents but were not confirmed in a current regulation text opened for this report. Fingerprint fee conflict (minor): older secondary sources cite $48.25 (superseded) per associated person. AMCO’s home page (accessed 2026-11-01) states: “Effective October 1, 2026, the FBI fingerprint processing fee has increased from $12.00 to $15.00 per submission. As a result, the combined state and national criminal history record check fee has increased from $47.00 (superseded) to $50.00 per submission.” Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/home.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01. This report adopts $50.00 per submission from 1 October 2026 as the most recent official figure and treats $48.25 (superseded)/$47.00 (superseded) as superseded. Verify whether the $50.00 figure is the total fingerprint cost for a marijuana applicant or only the FBI/state check component.

4.3 Each licence type in detail

4.3.1 Standard marijuana cultivation facility

  • Allows: commercial cultivation at more than 500 sq ft; harvest, packaging, storage, labelling; sale/transfer only to licensed retail stores, product manufacturers or other cultivators. The cultivation excise tax is charged at this transfer (see Part 2 of the full dossier; rates $50/oz mature bud, $25/oz immature/abnormal bud, $15/oz trim/remainder, $1/clone — confirmed in CannBus Q3 2026 synthesis of AS 43.61: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01).
  • Fee: $5,000 new (+ $1,000 application fee + fingerprint fees). Renewal: see conflict note in §4.2.
  • Canopy: no upper canopy cap is stated in the sources opened; the distinction from “limited” is simply “more than 500 sq ft.” Any upper plant-count or canopy cap would be in the operating plan / premises approval — Verify in 3 AAC 306.400-series and the facility’s approved operating plan.
  • Official info page: AMCO Marijuana License Application page (link in §4.2). No standalone “standard cultivation” info page was located; AMCO organises guidance by application form (Form MJ-04 for cultivation is listed on that page).

4.3.2 Limited marijuana cultivation facility

  • Allows: the same activities as standard cultivation, capped at no more than 500 sq ft of cultivation space.
  • Fee: $1,000 new (+ $1,000 application fee). Renewal: see conflict note.
  • This is Alaska’s low-cost entry tier. It is a SIZE tier, not a social-equity tier (see §4.9).

4.3.3 Standard marijuana product manufacturing facility

  • Allows: manufacture of marijuana products — concentrates, edibles, topicals and other infused products — for sale to licensed retail stores (and, per secondary summaries, transport in compliance with 3 AAC 306.750).
  • Fee: $5,000 new (+ $1,000 application fee). Renewal: see conflict note.
  • Secondary summaries state a food-safety permit is required for product manufacturing applicants. This was not confirmed in an opened official source for this report — Verify in the product-manufacturing supplemental application.

4.3.4 Marijuana concentrate manufacturing facility

  • Allows: manufacture of concentrates ONLY (not edibles/topicals as a product-manufacturing licensee could). Secondary summaries variously describe this as “solventless/limited-scope” production; the regulation text distinguishing permitted solvents/methods was not opened for this report — Verify in 3 AAC 306.500-series before advising on extraction methods.
  • Fee: $1,000 new (+ $1,000 application fee). Renewal: see conflict note.

4.3.5 Marijuana testing facility

  • Allows: independent testing of marijuana and marijuana products. A testing licensee may not hold an interest in any other marijuana licence (independence rule, reported in CannBus Q3 2026: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01; Verify in 3 AAC 306.600-series).
  • Fee: $1,000 new (+ $1,000 application fee). Renewal: $5,000 under pre-2025 text — the largest renewal/new gap of any type, and the type most affected by the proposed renewal-fee cut (see §4.2).
  • Operating labs (secondary, NOT an official count): a secondary testing-lab page states “testing-facility count Verify in AK-ACCIS (spelling CannTest vs Cannatest unresolved; the ‘only two’ count is removed).” Source (secondary, undated crawl): https://alaskastatecannabis.org/testing-lab — accessed 2026-11-01. Older sources name CannTest (Anchorage), Fairbanks Analytical Testing (Fairbanks), New Frontier Research (near Anchorage) and Southeast Alaska Laboratories (Juneau) at various dates, and Steep Hill (Anchorage, closed). The current operating-lab count is therefore Verify against the AK-ACCIS public search (§5.1) — AMCO does not publish a standalone testing-lab list in the sources opened, and CannBus Q3 2026 likewise states “operating labs: count not published in the sources read.”
  • December 2025 MCB action (secondary): the board voted to issue an immediate advisory on laboratory reporting of total THC. Source: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01. Text of the advisory was not opened — Verify.

4.3.6 Retail marijuana store

  • Allows: sale to adults 21+ and to registered medical patients (same stores, same tax treatment — no separate medical licence or medical tax exemption exists). Product scope in secondary summaries: flower, concentrates, edibles and infused products, subject to AMCO testing, packaging and labelling rules.
  • Fee: $5,000 new (+ $1,000 application fee). Renewal: see conflict note.
  • Operating hours / under-21 access: a September 2026 board packet (Administrative Order 360 project) proposed removing restrictions on retail operating hours and allowing accompanied persons under 21 on licensed premises, by repealing/rewording sections including 3 AAC 306.310 and 306.325. This was a PROPOSAL in a board packet, not adopted law as opened. Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/Portals/9/pub/MCB/Minutes/2026/9.22/TAB%203%20-%20Under%2021%20on%20Licensed%20Premises%20and%20RAAs.pdf — accessed 2026-11-01. Do not present the AO-360 changes as current law.
  • No delivery licence exists (see §4.1).

4.3.7 Onsite consumption endorsement — rules and how many are operating

  • Legal character: an ENDORSEMENT on a retail marijuana store licence under 3 AAC 306.370, not a standalone licence. Fee: $1,000 application + $2,000 endorsement fee (secondary fee tables; the $2,000 figure also appears in the 3 AAC 306.100(d) proposal document). Local government must authorise the business before it can operate (local opt-in); localities may allow indoor smoking only in a freestanding building with a separate ventilation system and a smoke-free monitoring area for employees, and outdoor areas must have a sight-obscuring wall/fence and pass a compatibility review (neighbourhood uses, air-intake vents). Alcohol may not be sold or consumed on site; non-marijuana food and beverages may be sold. No outside product may be brought into the consumption area. Sources: Marijuana Policy Project state-by-state consumption table: https://www.mpp.org/issues/legalization/state-by-state-on-site-consumption-laws/ — accessed 2026-11-01; 3 AAC 306.370 excerpts in the AO-360 packet (link in §4.3.6) — accessed 2026-11-01.
  • Consumption-area purchase limits (secondary summary of 3 AAC 306.370, NOT confirmed in an opened regulation text for this report): no more than 1 gram of flower, 25 mg THC in edibles, or 0.3 grams of concentrate (vaping only) per person per day in the consumption area; a September 2026 MCB package proposed removing purchase limits inside onsite consumption areas, but that package was not final. Sources: https://legalclarity.org/alaska-marijuana-laws-what-you-need-to-know/ and https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01. Verify the current 3 AAC 306.370(d)/(g) text.
  • History: Alaska was the first state to license onsite consumption; rules were signed 13 March 2019 (secondary). The first operating site, Good Titrations in Fairbanks (a former Chili’s restaurant on the Johansen Expressway, combining retail, concentrate manufacturing, cultivation and a café), opened 20 April 2021 after installing a ventilation system reported at ~$375,000. Sources: https://goodtitrations.com/about/ ; https://cannabisexaminers.com/alaskas-first-cannabis-cafe-celebrates-its-one-year-anniversary-local-business/ ; https://savingtheplants.com/what-ever-happened-to-the-promise-of-pot-cafes-in-alaska/ — all accessed 2026-11-01. Note the $375,000 figure is a press/operator-reported build-out cost for that one site, NOT a state fee or a general startup estimate.
  • How many are operating — CONFLICTING / UNVERIFIED: (a) Marijuana Policy Project (2026, secondary): “At least two retailers — Good Titrations in Fairbanks and Cannabis Corner in Ketchikan — allow on-site consumption.” Source: https://www.mpp.org/states/alaska/?state=AK — accessed 2026-11-01. (b) A 2026 consumption-lounge directory counts 5 in Alaska. Source (secondary directory, undated methodology): https://danklog.com/blog/cannabis-social-clubs-guide/ — accessed 2026-11-01. (c) A 2021–2023 press account states there is “no way to search for on-site consumption endorsements on a state website listing cannabis businesses” and that Good Titrations was then the state’s lone café. Source: https://savingtheplants.com/what-ever-happened-to-the-promise-of-pot-cafes-in-alaska/ — accessed 2026-11-01. (d) Harka Architecture (design firm, undated) calls Good Titrations “the only onsite consumption facility in the state of Alaska.” Source: https://harkahq.com/good-titrations — accessed 2026-11-01.
  • Treatment adopted: operating-endorsement count = Verify / Unavailable as an official figure. The only defensible statement on the evidence opened is: at least Good Titrations (Fairbanks) and Cannabis Corner (Ketchikan) are reported as operating with onsite consumption in 2026, and AMCO publishes no searchable endorsement list or count in the sources opened. Do NOT cite “5” or “only one” as an official count. Recommended follow-up: ask AMCO Marijuana Licensing (marijuana.licensing@alaska.gov, via AMCO home page) for a current endorsement count, or filter the AK-ACCIS public search (§5.1) if it exposes an endorsement field — neither was confirmed in this run.

4.3.8 Marijuana handler permit (individual)

  • Who must hold one: “A marijuana establishment and each licensee, employee, or agent of the marijuana establishment who sells, cultivates, manufactures, tests, or transports marijuana or a marijuana product, or who checks the identification of a consumer or visitor, must obtain a marijuana handler permit from the board before being licensed or beginning employment at a marijuana establishment, per 3 AAC 306.700.” Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MarijuanaHandlerPermit.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01.
  • Requirements (AMCO page, accessed 2026-11-01): approved handler-course certificate (from an AMCO-approved provider list on the page); Alaska name-based criminal history background check from a local Department of Public Safety office (NOT myAlaska), dated within 30 days of application; valid state/federal photo ID; one 2×2 passport-style photo or colour-printed selfie; $50.00 fee (debit/credit card, personal/cashier’s check or money order payable to the State of Alaska; NO cash). Applications in person (AMCO Anchorage office, Robert B. Atwood Building, 550 W. 7th Ave, Suite 1600, Anchorage, AK 99501), by email (marijuana.handler@alaska.gov — official public office address on the AMCO page) or by mail.
  • Disqualifying history (AMCO page summary): felony conviction in Alaska within the last five years (full list on the page; secondary summaries add: currently on parole for a felony, or certain Class A misdemeanours involving controlled substances or selling marijuana within the last two years — Verify the full list in 3 AAC 306.700-series).
  • Age: 21+ (secondary summaries; consistent with the establishment rules). No Alaska residency restriction is stated for handler permits in secondary summaries — contrast with establishment ownership residency in §4.4.
  • Validity: 3 years (secondary: Vangst 2026 state-by-state guide and handler-course providers). Source: https://vangst.com/how-to-work-in-cannabis-2026-state-by-state-guide/ — accessed 2026-11-01. Verify the 3-year term in 3 AAC 306.700, as AMCO’s handler page excerpt opened does not state the term in the lines retrieved.
  • Course cost (non-official, for budgeting only): e.g. one AMCO-approved provider lists ~3 hours online at $29.95. Source: https://cannabistraininguniversity.com/alaska-marijuana-handler-training/ — accessed 2026-11-01. This is a vendor price, not a state fee.

4.3.9 Other / related authorisations (not AMCO establishment licences)

  • Alaska state business licence: every business operating in Alaska needs a state business licence through the Division of Corporations, Business, and Professional Licensing (CBPL), renewed every two years (secondary summaries; see §5.6). It is NOT a marijuana licence and does not authorise marijuana activity by itself.
  • Local licences/permits: Anchorage, Fairbanks, Juneau and other municipalities impose their own marijuana business licensing/registration and taxes (see Part 3 of the full dossier). A state licence cannot be exercised in a locality that has opted out.
  • Hemp registrations: grower, processor, retailer and product endorsement through the Division of Agriculture — these are NOT AMCO licences and are covered in Part 7 of the full dossier; the Part 5/6 hemp data sources are inventoried in §5.7 below. Do not conflate a hemp retailer registration with a marijuana retail store licence.
  • Common carrier approval: AMCO publishes a list of approved common carriers for ALCOHOL transport (effective 1 January 2024 rule noted on AMCO home page). No equivalent marijuana common-carrier licence class was located — marijuana transport is by licensees themselves under 3 AAC 306.750-series (secondary). Verify.

4.4 Residency, ownership and transfer rules

Residency — CONFLICT that must be flagged, not smoothed over. - The regulation text (3 AAC 306.015, via Cornell LII, last amendment shown 22 January 2023, Register 245) provides, in operative part:

“(b) … the board will not issue, renew, or approve an application for a transfer [of] a marijuana establishment license to (1) an individual or a sole proprietorship unless the individual or proprietor is a resident of the state; (2) a partnership unless each partner is a resident of the state; (3) a limited liability company unless the limited liability company is qualified to do business in the state and each member of the limited liability company is a resident of the state; or (4) a corporation unless the corporation is incorporated or qualified to do business in the state and each corporate officer, director, and shareholder is a resident of the state.” “(2) ‘resident of the state’ means a person who is eligible at the time of application for the most recent permanent fund dividend under AS 43.23.” Source: https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/alaska/3-AAC-306.015 — accessed 2026-11-01. The same residency/PFD rule is repeated in a 2026 secondary brief: “every owner with a financial interest must be an Alaska resident eligible for the Permanent Fund Dividend (roughly one year of residency).” Source: https://drugpolicywatch.info/states/alaska.html — accessed 2026-11-01. - CONTRADICTING secondary summary: CannBus Legal Summary Q3 2026 states “Alaska does not impose a residency requirement on cannabis business ownership” and “No residency mandate.” Source: https://www.cannbus.org/lsum/ak_lsum.html — accessed 2026-11-01. - Treatment adopted: the regulation text is preferred over the secondary summary. Residency (PFD eligibility) for every person with a direct or indirect financial interest is stated as the rule, BUT marked Verify against the CURRENT 3 AAC 306.015 text, because (a) the Cornell text’s last amendment shown is January 2023 and a later amendment liberalising residency cannot be excluded on the evidence opened, and (b) a third secondary source claims non-residents may hold a stake in up to two recreational establishments — a claim that contradicts both of the above and was not traced to a regulation. Do NOT advise an applicant on residency without checking the current regulation and, if needed, AMCO guidance. - Other ownership rules from 3 AAC 306.015 (same source): a licence is issued to a specific individual, partnership, LLC, corporation or local government for a specific licensed premises; a person other than the licensee may not have a direct or indirect financial interest in the licensed business; the establishment must have a right to possession of its licensed premises at all times and may not lease its licensed premises to another person; a licence may not be transferred to another location without board written consent (and a transfer to a different local government is possible subject to AS 17.38 and 3 AAC 306). - Transfers of ownership: board approval is required; transfer applications appear regularly on MCB agendas (e.g. September 2026 agenda: licence 36831 BoomTown Buds, retail, Skagway, transfer within Francis, LLC; licence 10252 Popeyes Emporium II). Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/Portals/9/pub/MCB/Minutes/2026/9.2-9.3/September%202nd%20-%203rd%202026-MCB%20Board%20Meeting%20Agenda....pdf — accessed 2026-11-01 (via search result). Transfer application fee is stated as $1,000 in secondary fee tables (same as a new application) — Verify in the current fee schedule. The application process for transfers, including publication/posting requirements, is described for ALCOHOL transfers in AMCO guidance; the marijuana transfer form set (MJ forms) is on the AMCO application page — the alcohol-transfer 90-day publication rule must NOT be assumed to apply to marijuana transfers. - Ownership caps: NO statewide ownership cap (maximum number of licences per person/entity) was located in the sources opened. Secondary sources state no cap; the surrender/renewal data show single operators holding multiple licences (e.g. a 2024 comment letter refers to one operator holding six licences). Verify in 3 AAC 306 — absence of a cap in the sources opened is not proof of absence in the regulation. - Criminal-history bar for licensees (secondary): the board will not issue a licence to an applicant convicted of certain felonies or qualifying misdemeanours within the preceding five years; all owners/affiliated persons must clear fingerprint-based background checks. Source: https://www.cannbus.org/lsum/ak_lsum.html — accessed 2026-11-01. Verify the exact lookback and offence list in 3 AAC 306.025/306.035. - Local-government protest: under 3 AAC 306.065, interested persons may object within 30 days after an application is deemed complete and notice is sent to the local government (AMCO home page wording; some secondary sources say a 60-day local protest period — the 30-day figure on AMCO’s current home page is preferred, but the discrepancy is flagged as Verify). Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/home.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01.

4.5 Are licences capped or open?

  • State level: open (no statewide cap found; see §4.1 caveat). Applications are accepted on a rolling basis; the “deemed complete” list (§5.2) shows new and renewal applications being processed continuously, with objection deadlines in late October / early November 2026.
  • Local level: a locality may prohibit any or all licence types by ordinance or vote, and may impose its own caps/conditions (see Part 3 of the full dossier). A state licence is therefore necessary but not sufficient.

AMCO does NOT publish a current count of operating licences by type in the documents opened for this report. The figures below are the closest official figures available and must not be presented as a by-type operating census:

4.7 Onsite consumption — see §4.3.7 (endorsement rules + operating-count conflict).

4.8 Pending / proposed rule changes affecting licence types (NOT law)

The following were proposals or in-progress projects as of the sources opened and must NOT be presented as current law: - Renewal-fee reduction to new-licence levels (2024–2025 board proposal; see §4.2 conflict). - September 2026 MCB package (special meeting 23 September 2026): weekly employee sampling allowances; flower-to-concentrate-to-edible purchase equivalency (1 oz flower = 14 g concentrate = 11,200 mg THC in edibles); no purchase limits inside onsite consumption areas; fewer advertising restrictions. Status as reported: advanced by the board, then to Department of Law review and a 30-day public comment period; none final. Source (secondary synthesis of the board action): https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01. Note: the September 2026 discussion also recorded that the board “agreed to double” concentrate/edible limits but “did not formally vote, and the change is NOT in effect” (secondary legal summary, September 2026). Source: https://sativauniversity.com/cannabis-laws/alaska/ — accessed 2026-11-01. - Administrative Order 360 burden-reduction project: removing retail operating-hour restrictions, allowing accompanied under-21s on licensed premises/restricted access areas, rewording 3 AAC 306.310/306.325/306.705/306.710 (board packet August–September 2026). Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/Portals/9/pub/MCB/Minutes/2026/9.22/TAB%203%20-%20Under%2021%20on%20Licensed%20Premises%20and%20RAAs.pdf — accessed 2026-11-01. - Legislative audit of the ABC Board and MCB under way, report expected later in 2026 (secondary). Source: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01.

4.9 Social equity provisions

  • No formal social-equity licensing programme of the kind seen in some other states was located in AS 17.38 or 3 AAC 306 summaries opened. A 2026 secondary brief states this expressly: “Alaska’s cannabis statute does not contain a formal social-equity licensing program.” Source: https://drugpolicywatch.info/states/alaska.html — accessed 2026-11-01.
  • The closest equity-adjacent features are: (a) the low-fee limited tiers (limited cultivation $1,000, concentrate manufacturing $1,000, testing $1,000 vs $5,000 standard tiers) — CannBus expressly notes “This is a size-based fee tier, not a social-equity discount” (https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01); (b) the residency/PFD ownership rule, which keeps ownership local but also excludes non-residents (see §4.4 conflict); (c) home-grow allowances (see Part 1 of the full dossier).
  • No state grants or loans specific to cannabis businesses were verified: CannBus Q3 2026 states “AIDEA and the Alaska Small Business Development Center list no cannabis-specific program in the pages read.” Source: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01. Verify before advising an applicant that no assistance exists.
  • Record clearing is covered in Part 12 of the full dossier, not here, except to note that a 2026 petition-based record-sealing provision (HB 239, as described in a secondary brief) is NOT a licensing preference and must not be described as one.

4.10 Estimated startup costs (NON-OFFICIAL estimates — mark as such)

No official (AMCO/DCCED) startup-cost estimate was located. The figures below are weak secondary estimates and are included ONLY because the task asks for them, with their weakness stated: - Retail store (dispensary) opening cost: “$250,000 to $500,000” (covers rent, inventory, licences/permits, employees) — secondary site, undated in the crawl, and it incorrectly describes a “limited marijuana retail store license” which does not exist as a licence class (limited applies to cultivation, not retail). Source: https://finanssenteret.as/en/opening-a-dispensary-in-alaska-costs-and-considerations/ — accessed 2026-11-01. Treat the range as indicative only. - Alternative retail range from the same publisher family: “$250,000 to $750,000” for Alaska (and Colorado). Source: https://finanssenteret.as/en/how-much-do-dispensaries-make-in-california/ — accessed 2026-11-01. The two ranges from the same publisher conflict; neither is sourced to operator accounts. - Confirmed state fees for a new retail store (for the bottom-up floor): $1,000 application + $5,000 licence + ~$50 fingerprint per associated person + $50 per handler permit per employee. Everything above that floor (premises, build-out, security, Metrc, inventory, working capital, local fees/taxes, legal/compliance) is operator-specific. - Single-site build-out datapoint (NOT a general estimate): Good Titrations (Fairbanks) reported ~$375,000 to adapt its facility for onsite consumption, including an air-filtration system. Source: https://savingtheplants.com/what-ever-happened-to-the-promise-of-pot-cafes-in-alaska/ — accessed 2026-11-01. - Cultivation/manufacturing startup totals: Unavailable as a credible published estimate in the sources opened. Do not extrapolate the retail ranges to cultivation, where energy costs in rural Alaska, greenhouse/indoor build-out and the weight-based excise tax dominate economics (see Part 11 of the full dossier for energy context). - A 2024–2025 operator comment illustrates the recurring-cost burden rather than startup cost: one operator holding six licences cited $45,600/year in renewal costs alone ($7,600 per licence at the old renewal rates) plus “near a million dollars” in cultivation excise tax. Source: board packet letter in https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/Portals/9/pub/Archive26/MCB/Minutes/2025/3.14.25/TAB%203-%20Reduced%20License%20Renewal%20Fees.pdf — accessed 2026-11-01. This is one operator’s self-reported figure, not a market average.

4.11 Official info-page links (Part 4)

  • AMCO home / Marijuana Control Board: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/home.aspx
  • AMCO Marijuana License Application (forms, fee schedule, renewal): https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MarijuanaLicenseApplication.aspx
  • AMCO Marijuana Handler Permit: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MarijuanaHandlerPermit.aspx
  • AMCO MCB Meeting Documents (current two years): https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MCBMeetingDocuments
  • AMCO Completed Applications (deemed-complete list): https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/CompletedApplicationsforMarijuanaEstablishmentLicenses
  • 3 AAC 306.015 (licence conditions / residency) via Cornell LII: https://www.law.cornell.edu/regulations/alaska/3-AAC-306.015
  • 3 AAC 306 full text: via the Alaska Administrative Code on akleg.gov / the Lieutenant Governor’s AAC library — exact current URL Verify (the AAC is republished quarterly; the Cornell mirror used above shows amendments to January 2023 and may lag the current Register). All accessed 2026-11-01.

Part 5 — Licence Data Sources

PART 5 — LICENCE DATA SOURCES

Instruction applied: this section does NOT list every licence holder. It inventories every source of licence-holder data located, with URL, holder type, format, access/download method, update frequency as stated, latest data date, fields, and limitations. Where a direct download link or file name could not be verified from an opened page, that is stated — it is NOT invented.

Cross-cutting limitation (official, on AMCO’s own home page): “License and Permit Search. Here are the instructions and link for accessing the Public Search on AK-ACCIS. During the database transition, not all license information may be current. Please check in with AMCO staff if you have more questions or want to verify information on a license. Contact us at alcohol.licensing@alaska.gov or 907-269-0350.” Source: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/home.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01. Every AK-ACCIS-derived figure in this report inherits this caveat.

5.1 AMCO — AK-ACCIS Public License and Permit Search (interactive search, NO bulk download confirmed)

  • URL: linked from AMCO home page (“License and Permit Search” / “Marijuana License Search”) to the AK-ACCIS system at https://accis.elicense365.com (login via myAlaska; “Public User” path described in AMCO guidance). Home page: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/home.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01. AK-ACCIS login URL as stated in AMCO guidance documents: https://accis.elicense365.com — accessed via search result 2026-11-01.
  • Holder type: all AMCO marijuana establishment licensees + handler permit holders (alcohol licences share the same system; marijuana is a separate licence family within it).
  • Format: interactive web search only. NO CSV/XLSX bulk download button was identified in the sources opened. Whether individual record pages can be printed/exported one-by-one is Verify — do not assume an export exists.
  • How to access: follow the instructions/link on the AMCO home page; public search does not require a licensee login, but the system is reached through myAlaska infrastructure and AMCO warns (see cross-cutting limitation) that data may not be current during the database transition.
  • Update frequency as stated: NOT stated on the home page. Treat as “as updated by AMCO staff; no stated refresh cadence.”
  • Latest data date: not stated; the home-page notice itself was live on 2026-11-01.
  • Fields available: Verify — the search fields and record fields (licence number, DBA, type, premises address, local government, status, expiry) were not enumerated in an opened AMCO instructions page for this report. The deemed-complete list (§5.2) and board agendas (§5.3) show the field set AMCO uses in its own publications (licence number, DBA, licence type, local government, premises address, designated licensee, ownership percentages), which is the best available proxy — but it is NOT proof of the AK-ACCIS field set.
  • Limitations: database-transition currency warning (above); no bulk download confirmed; endorsement field (onsite consumption) not confirmed as searchable (see §4.3.7); Safari is not supported for AK-ACCIS per AMCO guidance (Chrome/Edge/Firefox supported).

5.2 AMCO — Completed Applications for Marijuana Establishment Licenses (“deemed complete” list)

  • URL: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/CompletedApplicationsforMarijuanaEstablishmentLicenses — accessed 2026-11-01 (via search result; page content retrieved in search).
  • Holder type: APPLICANTS (new + renewal) whose applications AMCO has deemed complete and which will be considered by the MCB — NOT a list of operating licensees. A licence on this list may still be protested, denied, or awaiting board action.
  • Format: HTML table on the AMCO website (no download file identified).
  • How to access: open the URL; lists are organised by the local government of the proposed/located facility. Full application copies are available only by records request to Marijuana Licensing (per the page: “If you would like to see a copy of the application, please send a records request to Marijuana Licensing and they will email you a copy”).
  • Update frequency as stated: not stated; the list is a live queue (objection deadlines roll forward).
  • Latest data date observed: objection deadlines of 30 October 2026, 5–7 November 2026 in the crawl (e.g. licence 25497 Five Star Flower, limited cultivation, Matanuska-Susitna Borough, objection ends 30 Oct 2026; licence 30593 Pipe and Leaf: Premium Alaskan Cannabis, retail, City of Fairbanks & Fairbanks North Star Borough, 30 Oct 2026; licence 12437 Alaska Cannabis Exchange LLC, retail, Municipality of Anchorage, 5 Nov 2026; licence 21227 Kusko Kush, retail, Bethel, 6 Nov 2026). This confirms the list was current to late October 2026 at crawl.
  • Fields: licence number, DBA, licence type, local government, public objection period end date (renewal section also shows these fields; new-application section uses the same columns).
  • Limitations: applicants only; no operating-status, expiry or premises-address field in the table as crawled; a licence that has already been approved disappears from this queue and must then be found via AK-ACCIS (§5.1) or board minutes (§5.3).

5.3 Marijuana Control Board meeting records (agendas, minutes, tabs/packets)

  • URL (current two years): https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/amco/MCBMeetingDocuments — accessed 2026-11-01. AMCO states it has “separated the last two years of board meeting documents from historical board meeting documents” and that archived documents are on a separate MCB Meeting Documents Archive Page (exact archive URL Verify — it was not opened for this report).
  • Holder type: all establishment licensees/applicants coming before the board (new, renewal, transfer, disciplinary).
  • Format: PDF agendas + PDF tab packets (one PDF per tab/item), organised by meeting date. No CSV/XLSX. No bulk download of a licence register.
  • How to access: browse by meeting date on the page; each meeting lists tabs (e.g. September 2–3, 2026 meeting: Tab 5 MCB Licensing Statistics 8/27/26; Tab 6 MCB Enforcement Report 09-2026 with stats; Tabs 7–11 disciplinary/NOV items; Tabs 12–16 new/transfer applications).
  • Update frequency: per board meeting (the board meets regularly; e.g. meetings 15–16 April 2026 in Nome, 24–18 June 2026, 2–3 September 2026, 18–19 November 2026 scheduled — the November 2026 meeting notice is at https://aws.state.ak.us/OnlinePublicNotices/notices/View.aspx?id=225552 — accessed 2026-11-01).
  • Latest data date: September 2026 meeting documents were the latest meeting packet set visible in the crawl; the next regular meeting was scheduled 18–19 November 2026 (after this dossier’s 1 November 2026 date).
  • Fields in agendas/packets: licence number, DBA, licensee entity + ownership percentages, designated licensee, licence type, premises address, local government, case numbers for enforcement (e.g. AM26-0377, AM26-1090). Licensing Statistics tabs are the single best official source for counts by type/status — but the CONTENT of Tab 5 (27 Aug 2026) was not opened for this report, so its by-type breakdown, if any, is Verify by opening that tab PDF.
  • Limitations: meeting-by-meeting snapshots only; disciplinary items show only cases before the board at that meeting, not a complete enforcement history; PDFs are not machine-readable registers (names/numbers must be extracted by hand).

5.4 Enforcement and disciplinary actions

  • Sources (all AMCO/MCB, PDF, no bulk download):
  • MCB Enforcement Report tabs in meeting documents (e.g. “Tab 6 - MCB Enforcement Report 09-2026 w stats,” September 2026 meeting) — see §5.3.
  • “Not Contested NOVs” packets (Notices of Violation not contested), e.g. June 24–25, 2026 packet: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/Portals/9/pub/MCB/Minutes/2026/6.24-25/Tab%2013%20-%20Not%20Contested%20NOVs.pdf — accessed 2026-11-01 (via search result). Fields in NOVs as crawled: licensee name, DBA, licence number, licence type, AMCO case number (e.g. AM26-0432, 26-0450), mailing/premises address, violation date(s), regulation cited (e.g. 3 AAC 306.740, 306.430, 306.715), fine amount (e.g. $100, $500). NOVs state they are “not an accusation or criminal complaint” and are public documents under AS 40.25.
  • Informal conferences / recommended disciplinary actions on board agendas (e.g. September 2026: Enlighten Gardens LLC, licence 21551, retail, Cordova — informal conference on recommended disciplinary action).
  • Holder type: licensed establishments (and, for handler matters, individual permit holders — handler discipline, if published separately, was not located).
  • Update frequency: per meeting / per NOV packet; no stated cadence for a consolidated enforcement register.
  • Latest data date: NOV examples dated April–May 2026 in the June 2026 packet; enforcement report dated September 2026.
  • Limitations: NO consolidated, searchable enforcement database or downloadable enforcement CSV was located. A complete disciplinary history for a given licence requires searching meeting packets meeting-by-meeting. Fines in NOV packets are the NOV-stage amounts, not necessarily final board-ordered penalties — Verify final disposition in the meeting minutes.
  • Tax-delinquency list (related enforcement-adjacent source): AMCO / Department of Revenue monthly tax delinquency reports (see §5.5 and §6.2). These list cultivators past due on excise tax (12 active + 37 inactive licences, $6.29M, 28 Aug 2026) but the report PDF URL/file name was NOT verified from an opened AMCO page for this report — Verify via AMCO or the Tax Division. Accounts in payment plans are excluded from the delinquency totals (per CannBus synthesis of the reports).

5.5 Department of Revenue, Tax Division — marijuana tax reports

  • Monthly report page (HTML, interactive): “Report of Marijuana Transferred or Sold” — URL as cited in secondary sources: https://tax.alaska.gov/programs/programs/reports/monthly/MarijuanaReport.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01 via citation (the page itself was NOT successfully opened in this run — see the gap note below; do not treat the monthly figures in §6 as directly re-verified from this page in this run).
  • Holder type: licensed cultivation facilities filing marijuana tax returns (the tax is levied on cultivators on sale/transfer, not on retailers or consumers).
  • Format: server-rendered HTML tables, updated monthly. A third-party audit of this page (Cannabis Data Project, accessed 2026-11-01: https://cannabisdataproject.com/alaska.html) reports: 117 consecutive months, October 2016 to June 2026, no missing month; two tables, seven columns each; DOR states reports post on the 10th of the month and prior months keep being updated for late/amended returns; quantity cells are published as the placeholder “#,###” in the monthly report (468 cells), so weights must be derived from tax ÷ rate or taken from the annual report — the monthly page does NOT directly publish ounces.
  • Download: NO direct CSV/XLSX download link was identified for the monthly report. The page is HTML-only as described. Exact file name: none (not a file download).
  • Update frequency as stated: monthly, posting on the 10th of the month (per DOR statement reported by the Cannabis Data Project).
  • Latest data date: monthly series through June 2026 as of the third-party check dated 16 August 2026; whether July–September 2026 months had posted by 1 November 2026 is Verify by opening the page.
  • Annual report data page (HTML tables): “Marijuana Tax Annual Report Data” — URL as cited in search results: https://tax.alaska.gov/programs/programs/reports/AnnualData.aspx?60000 — accessed 2026-11-01 via search result ONLY. Gap: a direct open of this URL failed in this run with a network-grant error (not a finding that the page does not exist or is login-gated). The annual figures quoted in §6.2 are therefore taken from the search-result rendering of that page plus corroborating secondary reporting, and are flagged accordingly. The page’s tables as rendered are: Collections Summary, Ounces, and Filing Information, with end-year selectors for 2022–2025 (2025 shown).
  • Format: HTML tables (no download file identified).
  • Fields (Collections Summary): tax collections, total tax (and, per the rendered snippet, the components that sum to total tax — interest/penalties are the likely difference; Verify by opening the page).
  • Fields (Ounces): bud/flower sold or transferred, immature/seedy/failed, trim/other parts, total ounces, number of clones.
  • Update frequency: annual (fiscal-year basis; Alaska fiscal year = 1 July–30 June).
  • Limitations: fiscal-year basis vs calendar-year press figures differ (see §6.2); collections (cash received) differ from tax calculated/liability by 0.4%–4.2% in the years checked (Cannabis Data Project); monthly quantity cells are suppressed (see above).
  • Spring Revenue Forecast (PDF/HTML): the Department’s Spring 2026 Forecast (13 March 2026) includes a marijuana line in the unrestricted non-petroleum revenue tables: FY2025 $6.8M, FY2026 $6.7M, FY2027 $6.4M — these are the GENERAL FUND share only (25% of marijuana tax revenue), NOT total marijuana tax. Source: https://tax.alaska.gov/programs/programs/reports/RSB.aspx?Year=2026&Type=Spring#program2027 — accessed 2026-11-01 (via search result). Confusing the forecast General Fund line with total tax is a known error — do not do it.
  • No Tax Division retail-sales publication exists: Alaska publishes NO statewide retail sales figure (see §6.1). Any “statewide sales” number is modelled from Anchorage local data, from the weight-based tax, or from third-party estimates — never cite one as a Tax Division figure.

5.6 Alaska Division of Corporations, Business, and Professional Licensing (CBPL) — business entity search

  • URL: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/cbp/main/search/entities (also rendered as /cbp/main/Search/Entities) — accessed 2026-11-01 via secondary guidance and AMCO guidance documents. Alaska has no Secretary of State; CBPL under DCCED holds these records.
  • Holder type: ALL Alaska business entities (corporations, LLCs, LPs, LLPs, nonprofits, cooperatives, ANCSA corporations) — NOT marijuana-specific. A marijuana licensee’s legal entity will appear here, but appearance here does NOT prove a marijuana licence is held or active.
  • Format: interactive web search (free, no login). No bulk download of a marijuana-filtered list exists; the search is by entity name, entity number, officer name or registered agent name. Filed documents for an entity can mostly be viewed/printed online at no charge from the Entity Detail page (“Click to View”); hardcopy/certified copy requests use Form 08-562 with a fee and 10–15 business days’ processing (per CBPL Obtain Copies guidance: https://www.commerce.alaska.gov/web/cbpl/Corporations/ObtainCopies.aspx — accessed 2026-11-01).
  • How to access: enter a full or partial entity name or entity number; open the Entity Detail page for type, status (active/dissolved/inactive/revoked), formation date (“AK Formed Date”), registered agent, officers, and filed documents.
  • Update frequency as stated: filings appear after processing (several business days’ lag is noted in secondary guidance; no official cadence stated on the pages opened).
  • Latest data date: live database (no snapshot date).
  • Fields: entity name, entity number, entity type, status, formation date, registered agent, officers/directors, filed documents. It does NOT contain: AMCO licence number, licence type, licence status, premises address for marijuana purposes, or tax standing.
  • Limitations: sole proprietorships, general partnerships and bare business-licence holders are NOT in the corporations database (separate business-licence search); dissolved/revoked/withdrawn entities remain listed with their status shown, so a hit is not proof of good standing; the search cannot be filtered to “marijuana businesses” — a researcher must already know the entity name/number (e.g. from an AMCO agenda) and cross-check.
  • Related: Alaska business licence search (separate tool, via CBPL) — a marijuana business must also hold a state business licence (biennial renewal). Its search URL/fields were not verified in this run — Verify via CBPL.

5.7 Division of Agriculture (DNR), Plant Materials Center — Industrial Hemp Program lists

  • Programme home page: https://dnr.alaska.gov/ag/akpmc/industrialhemp.htm — OPENED and read 2026-11-01. The page confirms: 11 AAC Chapter 40 effective 3 November 2023; forms for grower registration (11 AAC 40.200–40.295), processor registration (40.300–40.335), hemp product endorsement and labelling (40.400–40.420, including a separate endorsement application for marijuana product manufacturing facilities), retailer registration (40.500–40.525, including a marijuana-product-manufacturing-facility variant), and retailer records; plus an advisory notice on the prohibition of sale of certain industrial hemp products intended for human/animal consumption.
  • Downloadable lists linked FROM that page (PDFs, as labelled on the page):
  • “Current list of Registrants as of 10/5/2026”
  • “Current list of Endorsed Products as of 10/5/2026”
  • “Industrial Hemp Program Fees” (fee schedule)
  • “Alaska Accepted and Prohibited Hemp Varieties”
  • Application forms (grower, processor, endorsement, retailer) — the processor application as crawled is revision 2025.07.02.
  • Exact direct download URLs and file names: NOT verified in this run. The page text retrieved lists the link labels but the underlying PDF URLs were not exposed in the fetch. Do NOT guess them. Follow-up: open the DNR page in a live browser session and copy the PDF URLs/file names from the link targets, or request them from industrialhemp@alaska.gov (official public programme email on the page). The counts IN those 5 October 2026 lists were also not extracted in this run — the registrant counts in §6.6 are therefore older secondary figures, and the 5 October 2026 counts are Verify by opening the lists.
  • Holder type: hemp registrants only (grower, processor, retailer) + endorsed products (brand/product level, not holder level). Hemp registrants are NOT AMCO licensees and must not be merged into marijuana licence counts.
  • Format: PDF lists (per the DNR page’s link labels; the underlying files were not opened, so page count/fields are Verify).
  • Update frequency as stated: not stated on the page; the lists are dated snapshots (5 October 2026 is the latest snapshot label observed).
  • Limitations: snapshot PDFs, not a searchable database; no bulk CSV; hemp product endorsement is product-level and a single registrant may hold multiple endorsements.

5.8 Department of Health — Medical Marijuana Registry statistics (counts only)

  • Programme: Alaska Medical Marijuana Registry, Department of Health (Health Analytics and Vital Records; formerly Bureau of Vital Statistics / Division of Public Health). The registry list itself is confidential — no patient-level or holder-level list is published, and none should be sought.
  • Public count publication: NONE located on an official Department of Health page in this run. A third-party audit (Cannabis Data Project, accessed 2026-11-01) reports the Department’s sitemap carries no cannabis/marijuana entry and no patient count is published anywhere the audit could reach; CannBus Q3 2026 likewise records “the state registry page publishes no cardholder count.” The counts in §6.5 therefore come from secondary reporting of Department figures supplied by email, NOT from a published official table.
  • What to cite: patient counts ONLY, never names. See §6.5 for the figures and their conflicts.
  • Follow-up: request the current active-patient count directly from the Department of Health, Health Analytics and Vital Records section (the route the Cannabis Data Project identifies as the only way to close this gap).

5.9 Tribal records (public, if any)

  • None located for this report. No public tribal cannabis/hemp licence register, ordinance list or licence-holder list for Alaska’s 229 federally recognised tribes or the Metlakatla Indian Community was found in the sources opened. Tribal jurisdiction, operations and bans are covered in Part 10 of the full dossier from official tribal/government sources only; for Part 5 purposes the finding is: Unavailable — no public tribal licence-holder data source was located; do not assume a tribal register exists.
  • USDA tribal hemp plans: USDA’s approved-plans list includes tribal plans nationally; whether any Alaska tribe holds a USDA-approved hemp plan is Verify against the USDA list (see §5.10). No Alaska tribal hemp plan was confirmed in the sources opened.

5.10 USDA hemp records for Alaska

  • USDA-approved state plan list: https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/state-and-tribal-plan-review — accessed 2026-11-01 (via search result). The list was updated 17 February 2026 and shows: “Alaska | AK Approved Plan (pdf)*” — i.e. Alaska runs its OWN USDA-approved state plan (effective 1 January 2022, submitted 28 December 2021), NOT the USDA direct-licensing programme. Alaska’s plan PDF: https://www.ams.usda.gov/sites/default/files/media/AlaskaStateIndustrialHempPlan.pdf — accessed 2026-11-01 (via search result).
  • Consequence: Alaska hemp producers are licensed/authorised under the Alaska state plan (DNR Division of Agriculture), NOT by USDA directly. A USDA direct licence search should therefore return no Alaska producers as a matter of programme design — if it does, that is an anomaly to investigate, not a second Alaska register.
  • USDA Hemp eManagement Platform (HeMP) search: the law-enforcement search tool at https://hempsearch.ams.usda.gov requires USDA eAuthentication and approved access (request via farmbill.hemp@usda.gov); it is a restricted tool for law-enforcement/regulatory officials, NOT a public bulk download. A public HeMP search tool for basic licence verification is referenced in USDA/CSBS guidance, but its Alaska coverage, fields and export capability were NOT verified in this run — Verify before citing HeMP as a public Alaska data source. No HeMP download file or file name is given here because none was verified.
  • USDA programme contact page (Alaska entry): https://www.ams.usda.gov/rules-regulations/hemp/information-producers/grower-contact-list — accessed 2026-11-01 (via search result). Alaska contact as listed: programme page http://plants.alaska.gov/industrialhemp.htm (legacy URL; current page is the DNR URL in §5.7), email industrialhemp@alaska.gov, phone 907-761-3867 (note: this differs from the Plant Materials Center phone 907-745-4469 on the DNR page — Verify which number is current; both are official-public numbers as published, so both are reproduced here as published, not as personal numbers).

5.11 Local (borough/city) licence and tax records where published

  • Municipality of Anchorage — Finance Department marijuana sales-tax reports: Anchorage imposes a 5% local retail marijuana tax, filed monthly by retailers. The Finance Department’s monthly figures are the source of the Anchorage sales series in §6.3 (via Municipality reporting as relayed by The Marijuana Herald, October 2026: “The latest municipal report, dated October 7, includes sales figures through June 2026”). The direct Anchorage Finance report URL/file name was NOT verified from an opened municipal page in this run — Verify via the Municipality of Anchorage Finance Department (marijuana tax page). No Anchorage bulk licence-holder download was located.
  • Fairbanks (City + Fairbanks North Star Borough), City and Borough of Juneau, Bethel: local cannabis taxes are reported to exist (Bethel 15% is cited in secondary sources), but no direct borough/city licence-register URL, tax-report URL or file name was verified in this run. Verify each via the respective borough/city finance/clerk office before citing local counts or rates beyond Anchorage’s 5% (which is multiply corroborated).
  • Anchorage local licensing period: a secondary report notes the Anchorage Assembly extended the local marijuana licensing period to two years instead of annual renewals (Alaska Business Magazine cannabis archive, accessed 2026-11-01: https://www.akbizmag.com/tag/cannabis/). Ordinance number/date Verify — this affects how “annual” Anchorage local counts should be interpreted but does not change the state one-year licence term.
  • General limitation: local records, where they exist, are tax/registration records, not AMCO licence records. A business may appear in a local tax file without a current state licence and vice versa; the two must be cross-checked, not merged.

5.12 What does NOT exist as a downloadable Alaska licence file (explicit negatives)

  • No AMCO CSV/XLSX licence register was located (AK-ACCIS is search-only as far as verified).
  • No AMCO downloadable file with a verified direct link + file name was located in this run for ANY marijuana licence list. The task asks for “the exact direct download link and file name for every downloadable file” — the honest answer for marijuana licences is: none verified. The downloadable files that DO exist and were verified by label (not by URL) are the two DNR hemp PDFs dated 5 October 2026 (§5.7), whose URLs/file names remain Verify by opening the DNR page links.
  • No consolidated enforcement CSV, no handler-permit bulk file (only a dated “Active Marijuana Handler Permits” list linked from AMCO home, format/count unverified), no Department of Health registry table, and no tribal register were located.
  • If a future run obtains AK-ACCIS export functionality or a board-statistics tab with by-type counts, this section must be updated — the absence above is a finding about the sources opened on 2026-11-01, not a claim that AMCO holds no such data internally.

Part 6 — Market Data

PART 6 — MARKET DATA

6.1 The central measurement problem (read first)

Alaska does NOT publish statewide retail cannabis sales. The state taxes cannabis ONCE, at cultivation, by weight (AS 43.61) — so nothing in Alaska’s public data records what consumers spent at the counter. Every “statewide sales” figure in circulation is either (a) the weight-based tax itself, (b) Anchorage local sales-tax data grossed up or quoted alone, or (c) a third-party model. This report keeps those three things in separate tables and never presents (b) or (c) as a statewide retail total. Sources: https://cannabisdataproject.com/alaska.html and https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01.

6.2 State excise tax revenue and implied weight (official, Tax Division)

Basis: Alaska fiscal year (1 July–30 June). “Tax Collections” = cash received; “Total Tax” = collections plus the additional components in the Collections Summary table (interest/penalties — Verify the component split by opening the annual page, which failed to open directly in this run; see §5.5 gap note). The figures below are from the Tax Division Annual Report Data page as rendered in search results, corroborated by secondary reporting of the same page.

Fiscal year Tax Collections Total Tax Source note
FY2022 $28,353,379 $28,690,918 Annual Report Data page, via search rendering + Fool/MPP corroboration
FY2023 $29,129,302 $29,477,517 Same
FY2024 $26,963,435 $27,251,831 Same
FY2025 $25,399,436 $25,712,480 Same

Sources: https://tax.alaska.gov/programs/programs/reports/AnnualData.aspx?60000 (search rendering, accessed 2026-11-01; direct open failed — see §5.5); corroboration: https://www.fool.com/research/marijuana-tax-revenue-by-state/ — accessed 2026-11-01. - Trend: total tax fell from $29.48M (FY2023) to $25.71M (FY2025), −12.8% over two years (report calculation). Press reporting rounds the same story differently: “Total cannabis tax revenue FY2022 $28,690,918 to FY2025 $25,712,480; ‘$25.3m’ is Collections ($25,399,436) rounded, not the total” (MJBizDaily-style trade press, August 2026, via search: https://www.mmjdaily.com/article/9867533/alaska-cannabis-weight-based-tax-drives-growers-out-feeds-black-market/ — accessed 2026-11-01). The $29.6M/$25.3M pair is consistent with the fiscal-year totals above after rounding and after the collections-vs-total-tax distinction — but the press piece does not state fiscal vs calendar year, so the two series must NOT be spliced into a single time series without noting the basis change. - General Fund share (for scale, NOT total tax): Spring 2026 Forecast gives the marijuana General Fund line as $6.8M (FY2025), $6.7M (FY2026 forecast), $6.4M (FY2027 forecast); FY2024 actual General Fund share was $7.0M. Source: https://tax.alaska.gov/programs/programs/reports/RSB.aspx?Year=2026&Type=Spring#program2027 — accessed 2026-11-01. Revenue split as reported in 2022: 50% recidivism reduction, 25% drug/marijuana education and treatment, 25% General Fund (secondary; Verify the current statutory distribution in AS 43.61 / the appropriation acts — the split may have been amended since 2022). - Per-resident: U.S. Census Bureau Quarterly Summary (July 2025–June 2026, reported 10 September 2026) puts Alaska at “over $30” per resident in cannabis excise taxes, with no retail excise tax. Source (secondary summary of Census release): https://censuseasy.com/blog/state-cannabis-tax-revenue-2026 — accessed 2026-11-01. Population base used in that summary: 737,270. CannBus Q3 2026 likewise calls Alaska “more than $30, the third-highest in the country (Census QTAX estimate)” for FY2026. - Past-due tax (market-stress indicator, official via AMCO/DOR reports): $6.29M past due at 28 August 2026 (12 active + 37 inactive licences), up from $5.52M at 20 February 2026 and $4.4M at March 2025 (monthly series March 2025–August 2026 in CannBus Q3 2026, rising monotonically). Accounts in payment plans are excluded. Source: https://www.cannbus.org/cannmir/ak_cannmir.html — accessed 2026-11-01. - Monthly excise tax: the monthly series exists (October 2016–June 2026, 117 months, per the Cannabis Data Project audit of the DOR monthly page) but monthly VALUES are not tabulated in this report because (a) the DOR monthly page was not successfully opened in this run, and (b) the monthly page suppresses quantities (“#,###”) so any monthly weight series would be derived (tax ÷ statutory rate), not published. Recommended follow-up: open https://tax.alaska.gov/programs/programs/reports/monthly/MarijuanaReport.aspx in a live session, extract “Total Collected Tax” by month, and label it liability-vs-collections correctly (see §5.5).

Weight transferred (official annual Ounces table, Tax Division):

Fiscal year Bud/flower (oz) Immature/seedy/failed (oz) Trim/other (oz) Total (oz) Clones (count)
FY2022 329,691 239,014 471,230 1,039,935 8,162
FY2023 274,699 300,997 510,027 1,085,723 5,795
FY2024 185,745 372,984 572,952 1,131,681 4,009
FY2025 125,497 353,012 692,187 1,170,696 4,293

Source: Tax Division Annual Report Data, Ounces table, via search rendering — accessed 2026-11-01. The story in the weight data is the story of the market: total ounces ROSE while tax revenue FELL, because the mix shifted from $50/oz bud (329,691 oz in FY2022 → 125,497 oz in FY2025, −62%) to $15/oz trim (471,230 → 692,187 oz, +47%) and $25/oz immature (239,014 → 353,012 oz). Trade-press reporting of the same shift (“premium bud sales fell more than 70% between 2020 and 2025, while trim sales more than doubled”) is directionally consistent but uses a different base period (2020–2025, presumably calendar) — do not mix the two periods. Implied average tax per ounce (Total Tax ÷ Total Ounces, report calculation): FY2022 $27.59/oz; FY2025 $21.96/oz. These are report calculations from the official tables, not published Tax Division figures.

Implied sales — what can and cannot be said: No official statewide retail-sales total can be derived from a weight tax without a price assumption, and Alaska publishes no official retail price series (see §6.4). Any “implied sales” number would require multiplying ounces by an assumed retail price per ounce by product class — that calculation is NOT performed in this report because no official price input exists. Anchorage taxable sales (§6.3) are the only official retail-sales input, and they cover one municipality only.

6.3 Anchorage taxable sales (official local data, via municipal reporting)

Anchorage levies a 5% local retail marijuana tax. The Finance Department figures below are municipal figures relayed by The Marijuana Herald (secondary relay of official data — the municipal report itself was not opened; see §5.11).

Jan–Jun 2025 $52,123,564 $2,606,178 Via Herald, October 2026
